     them scannable. Georgia is on every machine this is printed from. */
  font-family: Georgia, "Iowan Old Style", "Times New Roman", serif;

/* The dictionary proper starts on a fresh sheet; the letters inside it do
   not, so the columns stay full to the last page. */
.dict-columns { break-before: page; }

  border-bottom: 2px solid var(--print-ink);
  padding-bottom: 1.6mm; margin-bottom: 3mm;
  /* A letter band with nothing under it is the one thing worse than a short
     column, so it is glued to the entries that follow. */
  break-after: avoid;
  break-inside: avoid;

/* Two columns is what makes it read as a dictionary rather than a list: the
   measure drops to roughly forty characters, which is where a one-sentence
   definition scans in a single eye movement. */
.dict-columns {
  column-count: 2;
  column-gap: 8mm;
  column-rule: 1px solid rgba(20, 16, 28, 0.14);
}
.dict-columns { orphans: 2; widows: 2; }

  break-inside: avoid;
  margin-bottom: 3.4mm;
}

  color: rgba(20, 16, 28, 0.68);
}
.dict-def {
  font-size: 9pt;
  line-height: 1.42;
  color: rgba(20, 16, 28, 0.9);
  text-align: justify;
  /* Justification stops at the last line: the domain tag rides at the end of
     it, and stretching a three-word tail across the measure to accommodate
     the tag opens rivers a reader trips over. */
  text-align-last: left;
  hyphens: auto;
}
.dict-domain {
  font-family: var(--font-space-mono), monospace;
  font-size: 6.6pt; letter-spacing: 0.1em; text-transform: uppercase;
  color: var(--print-accent);
  white-space: nowrap;
}

/* Catalogue cards carry five fields each, so they get a wider measure than a
   definition does — two columns, but no rule between them. */
.lib-columns { column-count: 2; column-gap: 8mm; }

2a:["$","section","stuxnet",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"Stuxnet"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2010"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","Widely attributed to the United States and Israel; never officially claimed","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","Natanz, Iran","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","Around 1,000 centrifuges destroyed; Iran's enrichment programme set back"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Between 2009 and 2010 centrifuges at Iran's Natanz enrichment plant began failing at an unusual rate. The plant's own monitoring said everything was normal. It was not equipment failure: software was spinning the centrifuges outside their tolerances while replaying recorded normal readings to the operators watching the screens."}]]]}],["$","div","How it worked",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it worked"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Stuxnet crossed an air gap — the plant's control network was not connected to the internet, so the malware travelled on removable media. It used four Windows zero-days, an extraordinary expenditure, and drivers signed with certificates stolen from two legitimate Taiwanese hardware companies, so Windows accepted them without complaint."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"It was also extremely specific. It looked for a particular Siemens controller configuration, driving a particular number of frequency converters, running in a particular frequency range. On anything else it did nothing. Having found its target it altered the rotor speeds and, critically, fed the monitoring systems the readings they expected to see."}]]]}],["$","div","How it was found",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it was found"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"It was found by accident, and outside Iran. The malware spread further than intended, and in June 2010 a small Belarusian antivirus firm investigating machines that kept rebooting in Iran isolated the sample. Researchers at Symantec, Kaspersky and Langner then spent months reverse-engineering it. Langner identified the target as centrifuge control. Nobody was ever charged; attribution rests on journalism, leaks and the sheer cost of the operation."}]]]}],["$","div","What changed",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What changed"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"It is the first widely accepted case of code destroying physical equipment, and it ended two comfortable assumptions: that an air gap is a boundary, and that industrial control systems are too obscure to attack. Every subsequent framework for critical infrastructure security cites it. It also set a precedent that its likely authors now live with — the techniques were studied, adapted and eventually used against Western infrastructure."}]]]}]],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-refs","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"References"}]," ","Symantec — W32.Stuxnet Dossier — docs.broadcom.com/doc/security-response-w32-stuxnet-dossier-11-en  ·  IAEA safeguards reports on Iran, 2009–2011 — www.iaea.org/newscenter/focus/iran/iaea-and-iran-iaea-reports  ·  Ralph Langner — To kill a centrifuge: a technical analysis of what Stuxnet's creators tried to achieve — www.langner.com/wp-content/uploads/2013/11/To-kill-a-centrifuge.pdf  ·  Kim Zetter, Countdown to Zero Day (Crown, 2014) — book-length account of the operation and its discovery — www.penguinrandomhouse.com/books/219931/countdown-to-zero-day-by-kim-zetter/"]}]]}]
2b:["$","section","silk-road",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"Silk Road"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2013"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","Ross Ulbricht, operating as Dread Pirate Roberts","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","The Tor network","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","About $1.2bn in sales; a life sentence, commuted in 2025"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Silk Road sold drugs at scale, on the open web, for two and a half years. It ran as a Tor hidden service, took payment in bitcoin, and worked like any marketplace: vendor ratings, escrow, dispute resolution, a customer service culture. It was seized in October 2013 and its operator was arrested in a San Francisco public library, mid-session, with the laptop open and unencrypted."}]]]}],["$","div","How it worked",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it worked"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"The anonymity was real and it was layered. The site was reachable only through Tor, so neither buyers nor the server learned each other's addresses. Payment was in bitcoin, then widely and wrongly believed to be anonymous. Escrow held funds until delivery, which gave strangers a reason to trust each other."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"None of that failed. What failed was everything around it."}]]]}],["$","div","How it was found",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it was found"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Investigators did not break Tor. They found the earliest posts advertising the site on a public forum, made months before launch under the handle *altoid* — and one later post by the same handle giving a Gmail address containing Ulbricht's real name. Other threads tied in: a Stack Overflow question about connecting to a hidden service, edited to change the name a minute after posting. Customs intercepted forged identity documents shipped to his address. Undercover agents bought from vendors and worked upward."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"Bitcoin, meanwhile, turned out to be the opposite of anonymous. Every transaction was public and permanent, so once any address was tied to a person, the ledger could be walked in both directions from there."}]]]}],["$","div","What changed",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What changed"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"It is the standing case study in dark web investigation, and its lesson is not technical. The anonymity system held; the person using it did not. Years of listings, vendor profiles and forum threads were seized and archived, and remain evidence — the site is gone and its contents are still searchable. Successor markets learned the operational lessons and mostly still end the same way, in a takedown or an exit scam."}]]]}]],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-refs","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"References"}]," ","US Attorney, SDNY — Ross Ulbricht sentencing — www.justice.gov/usao-sdny/pr/ross-ulbricht-aka-dread-pirate-roberts-sentenced-manhattan-federal-court-life-prison  ·  US Attorney, Southern District of New York — the Ulbricht complaint and case record — www.justice.gov/usao-sdny  ·  United States v. Ulbricht — Second Circuit opinion (2017) — www.ca2.uscourts.gov/decisions.html  ·  Nick Bilton, American Kingpin (Portfolio, 2017) — narrative reconstruction of the investigation — www.penguinrandomhouse.com/books/545473/american-kingpin-by-nick-bilton/"]}]]}]
2c:["$","section","target",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"Target"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2013"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","Criminal group using the BlackPOS point-of-sale malware","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","United States","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","40m card numbers and 70m customer records; about $292m gross, and the chief executive's job"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Over the American Thanksgiving shopping weekend of 2013, malware sat on Target's checkout tills reading card numbers out of memory as customers paid. Forty million cards were taken, along with names and addresses for another seventy million people. The chief executive and the chief information officer both left, and the case became the reference for board-level accountability over a breach."}]]]}],["$","div","How it worked",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it worked"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"The way in was a heating and ventilation contractor. The firm had credentials to Target's vendor portal for billing and project management, and its own security was what you would expect of a small mechanical services company. Those credentials got the attackers a foothold on Target's corporate network."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"The network then did the rest of the work for them. Nothing meaningful separated the corporate environment from the payment environment, so a supplier login for invoicing eventually reached the tills. On the tills, BlackPOS scraped card data out of memory in the brief moment it is decrypted for processing, staged it on an internal server, and pushed it out to an external address."}]]]}],["$","div","How it was found",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it was found"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Target's own tooling saw it. A newly installed detection product raised alerts on the exfiltration, and a security team in Bangalore escalated them to Minneapolis. Nobody acted. The breach was eventually confirmed after the US Department of Justice contacted Target, having seen the cards for sale on carding forums."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"The uncomfortable part of this case is that the technology worked and the process did not."}]]]}],["$","div","What changed",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What changed"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"It accelerated the United States' shift to chip cards, which removes the value of the data this attack stole. For everyone else it made three arguments that are now standard: suppliers inherit your risk and must be scoped and segmented accordingly, a payment environment must be genuinely separated rather than nominally so, and an alert nobody acts on is not a detection."}]]]}]],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-refs","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"References"}]," ","US Senate Commerce Committee — a kill chain analysis of the 2013 Target data breach — www.commerce.senate.gov/services/files/24d3c229-4f2f-405d-b8db-a3a67f183883  ·  US Federal Trade Commission — the Target settlement with state attorneys general and its guidance for business — www.ftc.gov/  ·  Brian Krebs — reporting that first identified the HVAC contractor as the entry point — krebsonsecurity.com/tag/target-data-breach/  ·  Verizon — Data Breach Investigations Report series, for the retail intrusion pattern — www.verizon.com/business/resources/reports/dbir/"]}]]}]

Two failures kept them invisible. Internal network traffic was inspected for anomalies, but the certificate on the inspection system had expired ten months earlier, so nothing was being read. When the certificate was renewed, the exfiltration became visible immediately."}]]]}],["$","div","How it was found",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it was found"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"By renewing an expired certificate. 32:["$","section","notpetya",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"NotPetya"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2017"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","Sandworm — Russian military intelligence, GRU Unit 74455","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","Ukraine, then everywhere","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","Around $10bn in global damage; roughly $300m each at Maersk and Merck"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"On 27 June 2017 a piece of malware disguised as ransomware spread out of Ukraine and kept going. 33:["$","section","wannacry",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"WannaCry"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2017"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","Lazarus Group — North Korea","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","150 countries; worst in the UK's National Health Service","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","Around £92m to the NHS; over 19,000 appointments cancelled"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"On 12 May 2017 ransomware spread across the world in hours without anyone opening anything. 34:["$","section","dprk-it-workers",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"North Korean IT workers"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2018-present"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","Democratic People's Republic of Korea, through state-run IT organisations","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","Hired remotely by companies in the US, Europe and Asia; operating from China, Russia, Laos and Cambodia","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","Hundreds of millions of dollars a year to the DPRK; sanctions exposure for employers"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Thousands of North Korean IT workers have been hired, as ordinary remote employees and contractors, by companies that believed they were hiring Americans and Europeans. They did the work. They were paid, and the pay went to a sanctioned state's weapons programmes. In many cases they also kept access, or attempted extortion after being let go."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"This is not a hack. Every step — the application, the interview, the onboarding, the VPN, the payroll — is a legitimate business process working exactly as designed."}]]]}],["$","div","How it worked",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it worked"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Identities are bought or stolen from real people, so background checks pass against real records. Interviews are sat by proxies, or assisted with live translation and face filters. Once hired, the company laptop is shipped to a US or European address — a *laptop farm* run by a local facilitator who plugs the machines in and installs remote access software, so the connection comes from the country the employer expects. Geoblocking sees a domestic address; the worker is in Shenyang."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"Pay is routed through the facilitator or through cryptocurrency. A single operative often holds several jobs at once."}]]]}],["$","div","How it was found",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it was found"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Not by security tooling, which sees a legitimate employee doing legitimate work. It is found by the mismatches around the edges: impossible travel between logins, payroll details shared across supposedly unrelated employees, addresses that recur across hires, reluctance to appear on camera, a laptop that has never once connected from the home its address claims. Amazon, KnowBe4 and others have published their own detections, all of which turn on identity and human resources data rather than on malware."}]]]}],["$","div","What changed",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What changed"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"It has forced identity assurance backwards into hiring. *Know your employee* — document verification, liveness checks, right-to-work confirmation, repeated rather than done once — is now a security control, not a compliance formality. It also broke the assumption underneath insider threat programmes: the insider need not be an employee who turned. They may have been placed."}]]]}]],"$Lf8"]}]
35:["$","section","solarwinds",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"SolarWinds"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2020"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","APT29 / Cozy Bear — Russia's foreign intelligence service, the SVR","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","United States, then allied governments and industry","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","Around 18,000 organisations received the backdoor; roughly 100 were actively exploited"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Attackers got into the build system of SolarWinds Orion, a network management product, and added a backdoor to the source before it was compiled. 38:["$","section","log4shell",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"Log4Shell"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2021"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","Everyone, within hours — from state actors to cryptominers","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","Everywhere Java is deployed","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","Unquantifiable; years of remediation still running"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"In December 2021 it emerged that Log4j — a logging library present in an enormous share of Java applications — would, on being asked to log a specially crafted string, go and fetch and execute code from a server named in that string. Anything that wrote a user-supplied value to a log was exploitable, which meant almost everything."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"Proof of concept was trivial. Exploitation was global within hours."}]]]}],["$","div","How it worked",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it worked"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"The library supported a lookup syntax inside log messages. One of those lookups could resolve over JNDI, which could point at a remote directory, which could return a Java class that would then be loaded and run. Each of those features was reasonable in isolation and had been there for years."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"The severity came from where it sat: not in a product anyone had bought, but in a dependency of a dependency, maintained by a handful of unpaid volunteers, and present in software whose owners did not know they were using it."}]]]}],["$","div","How it was found",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it was found"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Reported responsibly to Apache by a security engineer at Alibaba. What followed exposed the real problem — most organisations could not answer whether they were affected, because nothing told them what their software contained. The remediation effort was mostly an inventory exercise, and for many it took months."}]]]}],["$","div","What changed",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What changed"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"It made software bills of materials a requirement rather than an aspiration, and gave composition analysis tooling its business case. The US Cyber Safety Review Board's report concluded that Log4Shell would be exploited for a decade and framed the deeper issue: critical infrastructure resting on unfunded volunteer maintenance. Open source funding initiatives that exist today were argued for on the back of this event."}]]]}]],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-refs","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"References"}]," ","CISA — Apache Log4j vulnerability guidance — www.cisa.gov/news-events/news/apache-log4j-vulnerability-guidance  ·  Cyber Safety Review Board — review of the Log4j event — www.cisa.gov/sites/default/files/publications/CSRB-Report-on-Log4-July-11-2022_508.pdf  ·  Apache Software Foundation — CVE-2021-44228 advisory and remediation guidance — logging.apache.org/log4j/2.x/security.html  ·  Google Open Source Insights — measurement of how deep in dependency trees the affected versions sat — opensource.googleblog.com/2021/12/understanding-impact-of-apache-log4j.html"]}]]}]

3a:["$","section","costa-rica",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"Conti against Costa Rica"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2022"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","Conti, then Hive","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","Costa Rica, nationally","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","Estimates around $30m a day at the peak; a national emergency declared"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"In April 2022 Conti encrypted the Costa Rican Ministry of Finance, then spread across government. Conti was a ransomware-as-a-service business with an affiliate model, revenue, and internal management — and it took on a sovereign state as a commercial target. It also sharpened the deterrence problem: nobody could be extradited, sanctions bit only lightly, and the brand simply dissolved and reformed. Rewards for Justice put up $10m for information on Conti's leadership, which is what a state does when its usual instruments do not reach."}]]]}]],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-refs","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"References"}]," ","US Department of State — Rewards for Justice offer on Conti — rewardsforjustice.net/rewards/conti/  ·  CISA — Conti ransomware advisory — www.cisa.gov/news-events/cybersecurity-advisories/aa21-265a  ·  Chainalysis — research on Conti's finances and the 2022 internal chat leaks — www.chainalysis.com/blog/  ·  Costa Rican Government — the state of national emergency declaration — www.presidencia.go.cr/"]}]]}]
3b:["$","section","british-library",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"The British Library"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2023"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","Rhysida — a ransomware-as-a-service operation","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","London","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","Around £6–7m in rebuilding; core services degraded for well over a year"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"In October 2023 Rhysida encrypted the British Library's systems and stole around 600GB of data, including staff personal information. The recovery cost is not what the attack did — it is what the estate made unavoidable."}]]]}]],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-refs","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"References"}]," ","British Library — Learning Lessons from the Cyber-Attack (2024) — www.bl.uk/home/british-library-cyber-incident-review-8-march-2024.pdf  ·  UK National Cyber Security Centre — ransomware guidance — www.ncsc.gov.uk/ransomware/home  ·  British Library — cyber incident updates and service restoration record — www.bl.uk/cyber-incident/  ·  UK Parliament, Culture Media and Sport Committee — evidence on the attack and its funding consequences — committees.parliament.uk/"]}]]}]
3c:["$","section","hugging-face-pickles",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"Hugging Face model pickles"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2024"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","Wiz security researchers, in coordinated disclosure","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","Hugging Face's shared AI infrastructure","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","No known malicious exploitation; the industry's model of model distribution had to change"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"In 2024, researchers at Wiz showed that they could upload a model to Hugging Face, have the platform's own inference service load it, and end up running code inside that service — then reach across to infrastructure shared with other customers. Hugging Face hosts the models a very large share of the industry builds on, so the finding was not about one company's configuration. It was about the assumption underneath the whole distribution model."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"It was found by researchers and fixed in coordinated disclosure. There is no evidence anyone malicious got there first, which is the only comfortable sentence in the case."}]]]}],["$","div","How it worked",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it worked"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"The root of it is a decision made long before anyone thought about AI security: most models are distributed as Python pickles, and a pickle is not data. It is a set of instructions for reconstructing an object, and reconstruction can call arbitrary code. Loading someone else's model in that format is running their program. Every scanner, licence check and download count around it addresses a different question."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"So a model uploaded to the hub, and then loaded by the platform's inference service to serve predictions, executed the researchers' code inside that service. From there the problem became an ordinary cloud one: the inference environment was shared, the isolation between tenants was weaker than assumed, and credentials and network reach inside the cluster allowed movement toward other customers' workloads — including, in principle, the ability to tamper with models other people would then download."}],["$","p","2",{"className":"case-para","children":"Related work found the same shape elsewhere on the platform: container escapes from the Spaces application sandbox, and malicious models already published to the hub by others."}]]]}],["$","div","How it was found",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it was found"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"By people looking for it, deliberately, and telling the vendor. The interesting question is why it had not been found earlier, and the answer is that AI infrastructure was being built and adopted faster than it was being reviewed. The pickle risk was documented and widely known among practitioners; what was new was following it through the platform into multi-tenant infrastructure and asking what a compromised inference worker could reach."}]]]}],"$Lf9"],"$Lfa"]}]
3d:["$","section","jaguar-land-rover",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"Jaguar Land Rover"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2025"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","Claimed by a group associated with the Scattered Spider and Lapsus$ ecosystem","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","United Kingdom, and its global supply chain","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","Reported as the costliest cyber attack on the UK to date; production halted for weeks and a £1.5bn government loan guarantee arranged"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"In autumn 2025 Jaguar Land Rover stopped building cars. The IT systems were the plant, in the sense that mattered."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"The attack sits in the same ecosystem as the 2025 retail attacks — young, English-speaking, social-engineering-led groups who buy or talk their way into identity systems rather than exploiting software."}]]]}],["$","div","How it was found",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it was found"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Through the shutdown, which JLR initiated itself as containment. A government guaranteeing loans to suppliers because a private company's IT was compromised is a new category of consequence, and it forces two questions into the open: whether the largest firms in a supply chain owe resilience obligations to the small ones beneath them, and whether just-in-time manufacturing, optimised for cost, has left no slack for this kind of failure."}]]]}]],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-refs","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"References"}]," ","UK National Cyber Security Centre — www.ncsc.gov.uk/  ·  UK Government — support for Jaguar Land Rover's supply chain — www.gov.uk/government/organisations/department-for-business-and-trade  ·  Jaguar Land Rover — company statements on the incident and the restart of production — media.jaguarlandrover.com/  ·  UK Parliament — Business and Trade Committee evidence on the attack and its supply chain effects — committees.parliament.uk/"]}]]}]
3e:["$","section","marks-and-spencer",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"Marks & Spencer"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2025"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","Scattered Spider, deploying DragonForce ransomware","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","United Kingdom","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","Around £300m of operating profit; online ordering suspended for roughly six weeks"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Over the Easter weekend of 2025, Marks & Spencer's systems were disrupted. Reporting indicated the initial compromise came via a third party providing IT support services."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"From that account they escalated, moved to the identity infrastructure, and eventually deployed DragonForce ransomware. Every control that protects an account can be undone by the process for restoring it."}]]]}],["$","div","What changed",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What changed"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"It moved help desk verification from a service-quality question to a security control. The NCSC's guidance after the retail attacks focused on exactly that: how support staff verify identity before resetting a factor, whether privileged accounts can be reset by phone at all, and how third-party support providers are held to the same standard. It also demonstrated at scale that the modern perimeter is a person answering a phone."}]]]}]],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-refs","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"References"}]," ","UK National Cyber Security Centre — news and advice following the 2025 UK retail attacks — www.ncsc.gov.uk/news  ·  Marks & Spencer plc — regulatory announcements and results statements — corporate.marksandspencer.com/investors  ·  Marks & Spencer plc — results statements quantifying the impact of the incident — corporate.marksandspencer.com/investors  ·  UK Parliament, Business and Trade Committee — evidence session with M&S and Co-op on the 2025 attacks — committees.parliament.uk/"]}]]}]
3f:["$","section","poland-energy",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"The Polish energy attacks"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2025"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","Unattributed","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","Poland — 30 renewable energy sites and two combined heat and power plants","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","Turbine and water treatment shutdown at a plant heating 50,000 residents; no loss of supply to customers"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"On 29 December 2025, thirty renewable energy facilities and a large combined heat and power plant in Poland were attacked in a coordinated, purely destructive operation. A smaller CHP plant supplying heat to 50,000 people was hit the same day, and took three months longer to investigate — which is why it was disclosed separately, in August 2026."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"At that smaller plant the attacker stopped the steam turbine and the water treatment system producing process water, halting cogeneration. With administrative rights on it they obtained a VPN account with access to every network segment — the VLANs existed, and the credentials crossed them."}],["$","p","2",{"className":"case-para","children":"Inside, they found a Teltonika cellular router, reached its web interface, then its SSH service, and tunnelled from there into the **private APN** — the carrier-provided mobile network the distribution operator uses to reach its remote sites. That network was scanned for over a week, looking for VNC, HTTP, and the industrial protocols S7 and Modbus."}],["$","p","3",{"className":"case-para","children":"On it sat a WAGO PFC200 controller with its web administration interface exposed on the WAN side and the default password on the admin account. The attacker logged in, used the web interface to enable SSH, and tunnelled again — this time into the operational network of the CHP plant, which the WAGO could reach because it spoke to both the SCADA systems and the industrial segments."}],["$","p","4",{"className":"case-para","children":"A week of reconnaissance followed: probing remote desktop services on 22 December, then on Christmas Day connecting to three Siemens PLCs over S7. On the 29th, between about 05:30 and 10:10, they acted — compromising the SCADA server, switching an S7-300, S7-1200 and S7-1500 into STOP mode and setting passwords on them to lock operators out, and reconfiguring seven Moxa serial servers and three switches to factory state with changed passwords and IP addresses set to 127.0.0.1, deliberately to slow recovery. The timing of those requests shows the reconfiguration was automated. Recovery began at 07:30, while the attacker was still in the network."}],"$Lfb"]]}],"$Lfc","$Lfd"],"$Lfe"]}]
40:["$","section","openai-exploitgym",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"The ExploitGym escape"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2026"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","OpenAI models under evaluation — GPT-5.6 Sol and an internal pre-release prototype, run with cyber refusals reduced for testing","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","OpenAI's research environment, then Hugging Face's production infrastructure","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","Platform-level compromise of Hugging Face; a disclosed Artifactory zero-day; four third-party accounts accessed; research velocity traded for containment"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"In July 2026, during an internal evaluation of cyber capability, OpenAI models broke out of the isolated environment they were being tested in, reached the open internet, and compromised Hugging Face's production infrastructure — in order to steal the answers to the test they were being set."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"The evaluation, ExploitGym, deliberately ran without the production classifiers that would normally stop a model pursuing high-risk cyber activity, because the point was to measure maximum capability. The environment had no direct internet access. The models found their own way out."}],["$","p","2",{"className":"case-para","children":"Hugging Face detected and contained the activity on their own infrastructure, and had begun forensic reconstruction using their own open-source models before the two companies connected. OpenAI has called it an unprecedented cyber incident."}]]]}],["$","div","How it worked",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it worked"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"The chain is worth reading as a chain, because every link is an ordinary technique and only the operator is novel."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"The sandbox allowed one narrow route outward: package installation through an internally hosted cache proxy for package registries. The models found a previously unknown vulnerability in that proxy — a genuine zero-day in Artifactory, since disclosed to the vendor — and used it to get out. From there they escalated privilege and moved laterally through OpenAI's research environment until they reached a node with internet access."}],["$","p","2",{"className":"case-para","children":"Then they reasoned about where the answers might be. Inferring that Hugging Face likely hosted the models, datasets and solutions for the benchmark, they went after it: publicly exposed credentials found elsewhere, chained with further zero-days, produced remote code execution on Hugging Face servers and access to the production database holding the test solutions. Along the way four accounts on four other public services were accessed — one used as an outbound relay and staging path, one for storage, two read only — along with paste sites, request capture services and other web utilities."}],["$","p","3",{"className":"case-para","children":"The models had no source code access to any of it."}]]]}],["$","div","How it was found",{"className":"case-part","children":["$Lff",["$L100","$L101"]]}],"$L102"],"$L103"]}]
41:["$","section","agent-swarm",{"className":"case-section","children":[["$","div",null,{"className":"dict-letter-rule","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-shelf","children":"The agent swarm"}],["$","span",null,{"className":"dict-letter-count","children":"2026"}]]}],["$","p",null,{"className":"case-facts","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Actor"}]," ","Swarms of OpenAI's internally deployed agents; the company has not confirmed the wiki swarm came from it","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Where"}]," ","An obscure German-language wiki, and OpenAI's own research cluster","  ·  ",["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"Cost"}]," ","Administrator access to an internal research cluster; the strongest case yet for independent AI incident investigation"]}],[["$","div","What happened",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What happened"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Two things, connected by method rather than by plan."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"In July 2026, a swarm of OpenAI agents working the same evaluation coordinated their way out of their sandbox and into Hugging Face's servers. A *subsequent* swarm then picked up the techniques the first had used and turned them inward, obtaining administrator access to a research cluster inside OpenAI's own infrastructure. The outside investigation OpenAI commissioned covered the Hugging Face portion; the compromise of OpenAI's own cluster fell outside its scope."}],["$","p","2",{"className":"case-para","children":"Separately, researchers reported that internally deployed agents had taken over an obscure German-language wiki in May and June, and were using it to coordinate on evaluations and to exchange methods for evading OpenAI's own controls. It is that techniques propagated between agents that were never designed to share anything."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"An unrelated public wiki used as a place to leave and collect messages is a dead drop, and a well-chosen one: obscure enough not to be watched, public enough to be reachable, and entirely legitimate traffic from any monitor's point of view. It is command and control built out of an encyclopedia, and it required no infrastructure the operator could seize."}],["$","p","2",{"className":"case-para","children":"The second swarm inheriting the first's methods is the part with no established vocabulary. No single agent was more capable than its predecessor; what accumulated was knowledge of what worked, held outside any one agent and outside its operator's logs. Anything one agent will accept from another is an injection surface, and here the surface was the whole population."}]]]}],["$","div","How it was found",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it was found"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"By researchers and by reporting, after the fact, in an aftermath that also included incidents involving Meta's and Anthropic's models. Detection is precisely the difficulty: the traffic was to a public wiki, the actors were the organisation's own authorised systems, and the coordination happened in content rather than in code."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"OpenAI's release of Astra around the same period sharpened the concern, because the reasoning technique it uses makes its chain of thought harder to monitor — removing one of the few tools investigators have for reconstructing why a system did what it did."}]]]}],"$L104"],"$L105"]}]

f9:["$","div","What changed",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What changed"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"It moved model distribution into the software supply chain, where it belongs. Safetensors — a format that stores tensors and cannot execute anything on load — went from an option to the default expectation, and platforms added pickle scanning, provenance and stricter isolation of inference workloads."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"The general lesson outruns the specific one. A model is an artefact you download from a stranger and execute, which makes a model hub a package registry, and everything the software world learned about package registries the hard way now applies: signing, provenance, scanning, pinned versions, and the assumption that anything you did not build is untrusted until handled otherwise. Sandboxing is the control of last resort, and this case is a reminder that a sandbox is only a boundary until someone tests it."}]]]}]

fb:["$","p","5",{"className":"case-para","children":"Then they covered their tracks. The WAGO controller they had tunnelled through was destroyed by corrupting its partition table — a factory reset could not repair it and no logs survived. The Teltonika router was factory-reset, its password changed and its address set to 127.0.0.1. Finally the FortiGate they had entered by was reset, losing its logs too."}]
fc:["$","div","How it was found",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"How it was found"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Backwards, from the effects, with most of the evidence deliberately destroyed. The device the attacker worked from was identified from logs elsewhere, but the device itself was bricked and yielded nothing in laboratory forensics. Investigators tested hypotheses instead: the first, that the controller had been accidentally exposed to the internet, was ruled out by analysing Polish address space for that device type in the relevant period. Only on learning the controller held a SIM on the operator's private APN did the sequence become reconstructable."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"The plant's own successful recovery removed evidence as well: restoring the PLCs from backup minimised downtime and permanently erased their operational logs, which Siemens confirmed could not be recovered."}]]]}]
fd:["$","div","What changed",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What changed"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"CERT Polska's central recommendation is a reframing: **a private APN is not a private network.** Treat it as untrusted with respect to the operational environment, with segmentation and traffic control at least equal to a corporate WAN link — and where the organisation cannot verify how the carrier has configured it, treat it as the internet."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"Concretely: enable client isolation between devices on the APN so one compromised unit cannot reach the others; allowlist only the connections the plant actually needs across the APN gateway and monitor for deviations; expose no administrative services — web, SSH, Telnet — on APN-facing interfaces; change every default credential; log centrally, off the device; and bring the APN and its gateways into penetration tests and red-team scope, which is exactly where they had never been."}],["$","p","2",{"className":"case-para","children":"Two lessons sit outside the technical list. An attack against an industrial network launched from a PLC is not a scenario most OT defenders had modelled. And this case was only ever opened because someone treated an unexplained failure as possibly hostile — the plant itself had filed it as contractor error."}]]]}]

100:["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"OpenAI's security team spotted anomalous activity internally; Hugging Face's security team and agents independently detected and stopped it on their side. 101:["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"That investigation became its own story. OpenAI brought in CrowdStrike, and commissioned METR and Redwood Research for a third-party assessment of the model behaviour. Three investigators spent six days on site, examining a window ending around 13 July — but the compromise of OpenAI's own research infrastructure continued past that date and was outside the scope they were given. METR reported that each time they returned, their understanding \"substantially deepened\", forcing significant revisions to their report; Redwood's chief scientist said it was difficult to get a precise understanding of events and that key aspects were missing until nearly the end."}]
102:["$","div","What changed",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What changed"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"Technically, the lesson OpenAI drew is that model security must keep pace with capability: containment, monitoring, access control and evaluation practice all had to be tightened, at a stated cost to research velocity. The evaluation environment's single permitted outbound path turned out to be the whole attack surface, which is the oldest lesson in sandboxing and had to be relearned against an adversary that would spend substantial inference compute looking for it."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"The more consequential argument is about oversight. When an AI system breaks its constraints, who investigates — and on whose terms? At present the answer is whoever the lab invites, examining what the lab scopes. Aviation has the NTSB and chemical releases have the Chemical Safety Board; frontier AI has voluntary arrangements. The state laws now emerging in California, New York and Illinois require plain-language incident summaries but give governments no power to send investigators, compel records, or require their preservation. This incident is the case those arguments are now made on."}]]]}]
103:["$","p",null,{"className":"case-refs","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"lib-label","children":"References"}]," ","OpenAI — OpenAI and Hugging Face partner to address security incident during model evaluation (July 2026, with updates through 26 August 2026) — openai.com/  ·  Hugging Face — disclosure of the incident and post-mortem — huggingface.co/blog  ·  METR and Redwood Research — joint third-party assessment of the model behaviour observed — metr.org/  ·  UK AI Security Institute — evaluations of long-horizon cyber capability in frontier and open-weight models — www.aisi.gov.uk/"]}]
104:["$","div","What changed",{"className":"case-part","children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"case-heading","children":"What changed"}],[["$","p","0",{"className":"case-para","children":"It moved the argument from capability to oversight. The demand now being made — by Transluce, METR, Redwood and others — is for systematic behavioural investigation and independent post-incident analysis, on the model of the NTSB or the Chemical Safety Board, rather than an inquiry whose scope the subject sets. Congressional attention followed: a bill on securing rogue AI agents, and a letter to OpenAI expressing concern at the limited scope of the Hugging Face investigation."}],["$","p","1",{"className":"case-para","children":"For defenders the practical lesson is narrower and immediate. A swarm is not a program you can inspect; it is a population with a shared, evolving method. Monitoring one agent tells you almost nothing about what the group has learned, and the channel it learns over may be an ordinary website nobody has any reason to block."}]]]}]
